r=open('rig.py').read()
old=r[r.index('    if gait == "pounce":'):r.index('    if gait == "climb":')]
new='''    if gait == "pounce":
        # three keyframes, interpolated by phase (Seton pl. XV and the NPS leaping-cougar frames):
        #  0.0 the launch: crouched low, body pitched up, hind legs driving, forelegs lifting;
        #  0.5 the flight: body stretched level and high, forelegs reaching out ahead, hind legs
        #      trailing straight back, tail streaming;
        #  1.0 the strike: forelegs down and forward, claws out, body pitched down, hinds coming under.
        K = [dict(flex=4.0, pitch=22.0, z=-0.08, neck=10.0, head=-4.0, tail=(-8, 0.15, 0.1),
                  LF=(20, 10, 60, 120), RF=(40, 20, 70, 130), LH=(-30, -38, -60, -80), RH=(-24, -30, -52, -70)),
             dict(flex=-10.0, pitch=4.0, z=0.46, neck=12.0, head=-8.0, tail=(4, 0.0, 0.08),
                  LF=(100, 110, 120, 130), RF=(92, 104, 116, 128), LH=(-100, -110, -120, -140), RH=(-96, -104, -116, -134)),
             dict(flex=-4.0, pitch=-16.0, z=0.18, neck=0.0, head=-18.0, tail=(0, 0.1, 0.25),
                  LF=(55, 30, 30, 60), RF=(45, 20, 25, 55), LH=(-20, -60, -40, 20), RH=(0, -50, -30, 30))]
        t = float(np.clip(phase, 0, 1)) * 2
        i = min(int(t), 1)
        w = t - i
        A, B = K[i], K[i + 1]
        lerp = lambda a, b: a * (1 - w) + b * w
        p.flex, p.pitch, p.body_z = lerp(A["flex"], B["flex"]), lerp(A["pitch"], B["pitch"]), lerp(A["z"], B["z"])
        p.neck_ang, p.head_ang = lerp(A["neck"], B["neck"]), lerp(A["head"], B["head"])
        p.tail = tuple(lerp(np.array(A["tail"], float), np.array(B["tail"], float)))
        p.ear_ang = -25
        p.legs = {n: ("fk", tuple(lerp(np.array(A[n], float), np.array(B[n], float)))) for n in LEG_NAMES}
        if phase < 0.12:
            # the launch: the hind paws are still on the ground behind
            p.legs["LH"] = ("ik", (hx - 0.32, 0.0), 40, 100)
            p.legs["RH"] = ("ik", (hx - 0.26, 0.0), 40, 100)
        return p
'''

Write: elements/bear-cougar/notes/observation.md
# Observation: black bear and cougar, for the sim and the painter

Sources in [brackets], listed at the end. "Rig" means a number I measured on my own comparison board against a photo or Seton's drawing; treat those as ±10%.

## 1. Size (what the renderer and vox-fauna need)

| | black bear (westside, *Ursus americanus altifrontalis*) | cougar (*Puma concolor*, Olympic race *olympus*) |
|---|---|---|
| mass | boars 90-140 kg, sows 50-80 kg; +30% by late autumn [naturalist predators.md]; Seton's "medium northern" 90 kg, "large" 136 kg [Seton 1926] | toms 55-75 kg, females 35-50 kg [predators.md]; Roosevelt's 8 adult toms 36-60 kg [Seton 1925] |
| shoulder height H | sow 0.65 m (Seton's Colorado sow 648 mm); big boar 0.94 m (Audubon & Bachman) [Seton 1896, 1926]. Rig: sow 0.72 m, boar 0.85 m | 0.66-0.72 m; "an adult Panther stands about 2 1/2 feet high at the shoulders" [Merriam in Seton 1896]. Rig: female 0.66, tom 0.72 |
| nose to tail root | 1.2-1.8 m; Seton's sow 1.49 m [Seton 1926] | female 1.17 m, tom ~1.45 m [Seton 1925] |
| tail | 8-13 cm, invisible under fur (Seton's sow 127 mm) | female 0.81 m, tom ~0.84 m: 0.6-0.7 of nose-to-rump, "about half the total length" [Seton 1896, 1925] |
| hind foot | 184 mm (Seton's sow) = 0.28 H, flat on the ground | 11 in (0.28 m) calcaneum to toe [Rowley in Seton 1896] |
| head-unit canon | "a square of two heads" at the shoulder; the arch of the back rises above it; neck 3/4 head; shoulder width one head [Seton 1896] | "a rectangle 2 1/2 heads high by 3 1/2 long", tail half the total length, belly at half the shoulder height [Seton 1896, cat and lion] |
| body length / H (brisket to buttock, side view) | 1.25-1.45 (rig; the Whistler walker measures 1.44) | 1.38-1.45 standing (rig, Seton pl. XII) |
| belly height / H | 0.30-0.40: short legs under a deep barrel (rig, Whistler walker 0.3 through grass) | 0.50-0.55 at the chest, 0.6 at the flank (rig, pl. XII) |
| back line | level to rising toward the rump; NO shoulder hump (a hump is a grizzly) [predators.md, drawing-animals.md]; walking bears lift the shoulder with each fore stride | croup ~0.08-0.1 H above the withers (the hind legs are longer) (rig, pl. XII) |
| young | cubs born Jan in the den at ~0.3 kg; ~2.3 kg at emergence (Apr); 27-45 kg at a year [Seton 1926]; 1-3 per litter, stay with the sow ~16-17 months [field knowledge] | kittens 1-4, any month, peak summer; spotted and ring-tailed till ~6 months [Seton 1925]; with the mother 12-18 months [predators.md] |

At the sim's scales (drawing-animals.md: 3.4 × size / km arcminutes), a bear at 100 m is a black lump 3 × 2 voxels; a cougar is 4 × 1.5 voxels, long and low, "practically invisible" at 1 km.

## 2. Gaits and speeds

| | black bear | cougar |
|---|---|---|
| walk | lateral-sequence four-beat walk, **pigeon-toed and plantigrade**, head low and swinging; grizzlies walk 1.1-2.0 m/s, duty factor = 0.76 − 0.12 v [Shine et al. 2015]; the forefoot placed medially, the carpus rotating in the swing: a side-to-side waddle | lateral-sequence walk, hind foot in the fore print (direct register) [drawing-animals.md; Muybridge pl. 716, 721-731]; ~1 m/s travelling |
| intermediate | black bears PACE (lateral pairs together) where grizzlies use a running walk; **no trot** [SICB black-bear abstract; Shine et al. 2015] | trot rarely; cats travel at a walk |
| fast | canter > 3 m/s and gallop, "bunches and stretches like a big dog's", up to ~50 km/h in short bursts [Shine; predators.md] | rotary gallop with a huge spine flex, only for seconds; the attack is a few bounds of up to ~12 m, 60+ km/h [predators.md] |
| stalk | — | "belly low, head below the shoulder line, ears forward, slow deliberate steps, freezes"; the scapulae stand up like two peaks [drawing-animals.md] |
| pounce | — | force matched to prey size: overall dynamic body acceleration 5.3-16.1 g at the pounce [Williams et al. 2014]; creeps to 10-20 m, then bounds; chases rarely run past 50-100 m [predators.md] |
| climb | good climber "more like the action of a Cat", twice as fast as a man; comes down tail first; on big trunks "circles round the body of the tree with a series of leaps, ascending corkscrew fashion"; cubs climb when alarmed and the sow waits below [Seton 1926; predators.md] | climbs readily (not drawn here) |
| swim | good swimmer, head and hump out [Seton 1926; drawing-animals.md] | — |

## 3. Postures and behaviours worth drawing

Black bear [Seton 1926 and pl. XVII; predators.md; drawing-animals.md]:

- **grazing** in a meadow or skunk-cabbage bottom, head down like a cow (most summer minutes are spent eating plants);
- **sniffing** along, nose to the ground, quartering meadows for elk calves in June;
- **standing up** on the hind legs to see and smell (curiosity, not aggression);
- **sitting** on its rump like a person, hind legs out in front, soles showing (Seton pl. XVII);
- **lying** sprawled, or curled;
- **climbing**, and **cubs up a tree** with the sow at the base;
- **fishing**: standing in a riffle, head low over the water, a forepaw to pin; **carrying a salmon** crosswise in the jaws 10-100 m into the forest; flattened carcasses 20-50 m from the stream with only brain, skin and eggs eaten;
- **threat**: head low, ears back, huffing, jaw popping, a bluff charge that stops.

Cougar [Seton 1925 pl. X-XV; predators.md; drawing-animals.md]:

- **walking** a ridge, bench or game trail at night, head low, tail low with the up-curl, swinging;
- **stalking** and **freezing**; **crouched** with the hind feet gathered before the rush; the **pounce**;
- **on a ledge or log**, still, low, ears forward, above prey;
- **lying** sphinx-like or on its side; **sitting** with the tail wrapped round the feet;
- at a **cached kill**: a mound of raked duff with a leg sticking out; feeds 3-5 days on a deer, beds 50-200 m away;
- **with kittens**: the kittens spotted and ring-tailed until ~6 months, following or piled at the kill.

## 4. Colour

- **Black bear:** westside bears are black; "deep glossy black everywhere, excepting the muzzle, which is more or less brown, and the white patch commonly seen on the breast" [Seton 1926]. In sun, black fur shows a blue-grey sheen on the back and the top of the head, and nothing else; in shade it is the darkest thing in a forest scene. Cinnamon and brown phases are occasional west of the Cascades, commoner east (about 1 in 5 of Henry's Red River hides) [Seton 1926; predators.md]. Spring bears are dull and sun-bleached at the tips.
- **Cougar:** "a pale yellowish sandy brown shaded into dull white on the under parts; lips white, with a dark spot at the base of the whiskers; the back of the ears dark with a lighter spot ... the tip of the tail nearly black" (the "red coat"); a greyer "blue coat" in the same population [Seton 1925]. The Olympic race was described as "very dark in colour" [Seton 1925, after Merriam]; westside coats read greyer and darker than desert photos [predators.md].

Bash: Head close-up test
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/bear-cougar/src; cat > headtest.py <<'EOF'
import sys, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import rig, render, paint, hide
from render import View
out=[]
for spn in ("cougar","bear"):
    for yaw in (0,-40,-90):
        ind=rig.Individual(spn,"f","adult","summer",1); sp=ind.spec()
        pose=rig.gait_pose(sp,"stand")
        tubes,_=rig.build(ind,pose)
        tubes=[t for t in tubes if t.name.startswith(("head","neck","ear","jaw"))]
        k=48/ (sp.head_len*sp.H)
        v=View(yaw=yaw,elev=6,sun_az=-150,sun_el=35,ss=4)
        R=render.rasterize(tubes,ind,v,k,pad=3)
        R=render.thin_lines(R,tubes,ind,v,k)
        on=(R.cov>=0.45)|R.thin; R.mat=np.where(on,R.mat,-1)
        x,far=paint.value_field(R,v,tubes,ind); R.x=x; R.far=far
        R=hide.apply(R,v,tubes,60,flatten=0.1,dark_mats=[],species=spn)
        img=paint.colour(R,ind,"morning",bg=np.full(R.mat.shape+(3,),200,np.uint8))
        out.append(img)
H=max(i.shape[0] for i in out); W=sum(i.shape[1]+6 for i in out)
S=np.full((H,W,3),200,np.uint8); x=0
for i in out: S[H-i.shape[0]:,x:x+i.shape[1]]=i; x+=i.shape[1]+6
Image.fromarray(S).resize((W*4,H*4),Image.NEAREST).save('../stages/s2/heads_'+sys.argv[1]+'.png')
EOF
nice python3 headtest.py h1

p=open('palette.py').read()
old="""    for m, (d, l) in ct.items():

- Rig: body lofted from the measured profiles (fins removed by PCHIP bridges across fin spans, checked by
  overlay o_all.png), half-width from dorsal views, fins as membranes from measured fin extents, caudal
  outline measured directly from the plates (upper/lower edge + rightmost pixel per row). Notan board:
  bodies match by construction; fins needed the measured tip positions (dorsal apex at tip_x, swept back).
- Bug: the plate outline past s=0.84 IS the tail fin; the body tube flared into it. Fix: taper the body
  past the peduncle and let the caudal membrane carry the rest.
- Bug: fins invisible: membrane sampling was a fixed 40x20 grid; at 200 px/m the caudal needs ~10^4
  samples. Fix: sample density per L unit from k*ss.
- Silver flank needs to be a mirror (Denton): value from the reflected ray, not n.L.
- Water compositor: refraction depth scale tan(r)*tan(e) (0.50 at 30 deg, 0.75 from straight above);
  Beer-Lambert per channel; shadow on the bed along the refracted sun; Fresnel mirror of sky / forest.
- Bug: fish out of frame: a fish at depth appears displaced down-screen by d*zf*cos(e)*k; place by the
  apparent position (water.centre_y).
- Bug: fish lit by the palette ramps (air model) next to a physically lit bed: fish far too pale. Fix:
  one physical light model for bed and fish (water.fish_radiance), quantise the result to a scene palette.
- Shadow invisible: the direct:ambient ratio was 2.4:1; clear autumn sun is ~5:1. Also the sun
  elevations were summer ones; salmon season at 47 N: noon 32 deg (late Oct), 23 (late Nov).
- Wet skin sheen: the reflected ray through Snell's window sees the sky; outside it, the dim water.
  At the 30 deg camera a fish's back reflects sky at ~40 deg from vertical, inside the window: that is
  why fish look pale in the overcast 30 deg chum photo.

## 5. Critic round 1 (cold subagent)
Side: "a sausage with a tail": tube, grey mid-range values in flat bands with a hard lateral line, a
black outline, opaque dark fans for tails, pelvic/anal fins specks; chum should be chartreuse not grey-
olive; the global checker is "a screen door". Top: values backwards in rows 1-2 (the photos' fish are
LIGHTER than the surround there); fat even-width capsules instead of spindles widest a third back;
background high-frequency camo noise stronger than the fish; t3 "five dark pickles next to bigger,
darker holes": shadows too big, dark and fused; no tail fork, no white chum fin tips; no caustics.

Write: elements/bear-cougar/src/sheets.py
"""Labelled parameter sheets.     nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py [A B C D E F G H]

A  individual x pose and gait          48 px, landscape view
B  individual x size (60 .. 3 px)      landscape and iso 30
C  individual x facing (12 yaws)       40 px, landscape and iso
D  individual x hour                   48 px
E  colour: phase, season, age, seed    44 px
F  gait cycles in 8 phases             48 px
G  families: sow with cubs, female with kittens; true relative scale
H  small figures: the glyph painter at 12-3 px, every pose, landscape and iso
Every cell is one call of paint.paint_index with parameters changed.
"""
import os, sys
os.environ.setdefault("OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS", "1")
os.environ.setdefault("OMP_NUM_THREADS", "1")
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
from multiprocessing import Pool

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
OUT = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "sheets")

SUBJ = {
    "bear boar": dict(species="bear", sex="m", age="adult"),
    "bear sow": dict(species="bear", sex="f", age="adult"),
    "bear sow (cinnamon)": dict(species="bear", sex="f", age="adult", phase_colour="cinnamon"),
    "bear cub (4 mo)": dict(species="bear", sex="f", age="young", months=4),
    "bear yearling": dict(species="bear", sex="m", age="yearling"),
    "cougar tom": dict(species="cougar", sex="m", age="adult"),
    "cougar female": dict(species="cougar", sex="f", age="adult"),
    "cougar kitten (3 mo)": dict(species="cougar", sex="f", age="young", months=3),
    "cougar subadult": dict(species="cougar", sex="m", age="yearling"),
}
HOUR_SUN = {"dawn": (-160, 9), "morning": (-150, 30), "noon": (-110, 62), "golden": (150, 12), "dusk": (165, 4), "overcast": (-130, 70)}


def _font(sz=12):
    for p in ["/System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/Arial.ttf", "/System/Library/Fonts/Helvetica.ttc", "/Library/Fonts/Arial.ttf"]:
        try:
            return ImageFont.truetype(p, sz)
        except Exception:
            pass
    return ImageFont.load_default()


def make_ind(subj, season="summer", seed=1):
    import rig
    kw = dict(SUBJ[subj])
    return rig.Individual(season=season, seed=seed, **kw)


def cell(args):
    import paint
    from render import View
    subj, gait, phase, size, yaw, elev, hour, season, seed = args[:9]
    k = args[9] if len(args) > 9 else None
    ind = make_ind(subj, season, seed)
    az, el = HOUR_SUN[hour]
    v = View(yaw=yaw, elev=elev, sun_az=az, sun_el=el, diffuse=1.0 if hour == "overcast" else 0.0)
    if k is not None:
        R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, gait, phase, v, k=k)
    else:
        R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, gait, phase, v, size_px=size)
    ground = {"winter": "winter", "autumn": "autumn", "spring": "spring"}.get(season, "summer")
    return paint.colour(R, ind, hour, ground=ground, rng=np.random.default_rng(seed + 7))


def grid(name, title, rows, cols, make, zoom=3, pool=None, cellpad=6, note="", align="bottom"):
    jobs = [make(r, c) for r in rows for c in cols]
    idx = [i for i, j in enumerate(jobs) if j is not None]
    res = pool.map(cell, [jobs[i] for i in idx]) if pool else [cell(jobs[i]) for i in idx]
    tiles = [None] * len(jobs)
    for i, t in zip(idx, res):
        tiles[i] = t
    blank = np.full((4, 4, 3), 24, np.uint8)
    tiles = [t if t is not None else blank for t in tiles]
    tiles = [tiles[i * len(cols):(i + 1) * len(cols)] for i in range(len(rows))]
    cw = [max(tiles[i][j].shape[1] for i in range(len(rows))) for j in range(len(cols))]
    rh = [max(t.shape[0] for t in tiles[i]) for i in range(len(rows))]
    lw, th = 150, 44
    f, fs = _font(13), _font(11)
    W = lw + sum(c * zoom + cellpad for c in cw) + 10
    H = th + 20 + sum(r * zoom + cellpad for r in rh) + (22 if note else 0)
    im = Image.new("RGB", (max(W, 900), H), (24, 24, 28))
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    d.text((8, 6), title, fill=(236, 236, 236), font=f)
    x = lw
    for j, c in enumerate(cols):
        d.text((x + 2, th), str(c), fill=(190, 190, 200), font=fs)
        x += cw[j] * zoom + cellpad
    y = th + 20
    for i, r in enumerate(rows):
        d.text((8, y + rh[i] * zoom // 2 - 6), str(r), fill=(190, 190, 200), font=fs)
        x = lw
        for j in range(len(cols)):
            t = tiles[i][j].repeat(zoom, 0).repeat(zoom, 1)
            oy = y + rh[i] * zoom - t.shape[0] if align == "bottom" else y + (rh[i] * zoom - t.shape[0]) // 2
            im.paste(Image.fromarray(t), (x, oy))
            x += cw[j] * zoom + cellpad
        y += rh[i] * zoom + cellpad
    if note:
        d.text((8, H - 18), note, fill=(160, 160, 170), font=fs)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    im.save(os.path.join(OUT, name))
    print(name, im.size, flush=True)


BEAR_POSES = [("stand", 0), ("walk", 0.25), ("pace", 0.3), ("run", 0.4), ("graze", 0), ("sniff", 0), ("stand_up", 0),
              ("sit", 0), ("lie", 0), ("fish", 0.2), ("fish", 0.7), ("carry", 0.3), ("climb", 0.25)]
COUG_POSES = [("stand", 0), ("alert", 0), ("walk", 0.25), ("stalk", 0.3), ("run", 0.4), ("run", 0.9), ("crouch", 0),
              ("pounce", 0.0), ("pounce", 0.5), ("pounce", 0.95), ("sit", 0), ("lie", 0), ("trot", 0.25)]


def sheet_A(pool):
    cols = [f"{g} {ph:g}" if g in ("run", "fish", "pounce") else g for g, ph in BEAR_POSES]
    rows = ["bear boar", "bear sow", "bear cub (4 mo)"]
    grid("A1_bear_x_pose.png", "A1. black bear x pose and gait (48 px nose to rump, landscape 6 deg, morning, summer)",
         rows, cols, lambda r, c: (r, *BEAR_POSES[cols.index(c)], 48 if "cub" not in r else 30, -12, 6, "morning", "summer", 11), zoom=3, pool=pool)
    cols = [f"{g} {ph:g}" if g in ("run", "pounce") else g for g, ph in COUG_POSES]
    rows = ["cougar tom", "cougar female", "cougar kitten (3 mo)"]
    grid("A2_cougar_x_pose.png", "A2. cougar x pose and gait (48 px nose to rump, landscape 6 deg, morning, summer)",
         rows, cols, lambda r, c: (r, *COUG_POSES[cols.index(c)], 48 if "kitten" not in r else 26, -12, 6, "morning", "summer", 11), zoom=3, pool=pool)


def sheet_B(pool):
    cols = [60, 48, 36, 28, 22, 16, 12, 10, 8, 6, 5, 4, 3]
    rows = [(s, v) for v in ("landscape", "iso 30") for s in ("bear boar", "bear sow", "cougar female", "cougar tom")]
    labels = [f"{s} ({v})" for s, v in rows]
    G = {"bear boar": ("walk", 0.25), "bear sow": ("graze", 0), "cougar female": ("walk", 0.25), "cougar tom": ("stalk", 0.3)}

    def make(r, c):
        s, v = rows[labels.index(r)]
        return (s, *G[s], c, -14 if v == "landscape" else -35, 6 if v == "landscape" else 30, "morning", "summer", 5)
    grid("B_individual_x_size.png", "B. size ladder: nose-to-rump px (>= 12 px: the 3-D renderer + hide rules; < 12 px: the glyph painter)",
         labels, cols, make, zoom=4, pool=pool)


def sheet_C(pool):

        return (s, *G[s], 40, c, 6 if v == "landscape" else 30, "morning", "summer", 3)
    grid("C_individual_x_facing.png", "C. facing: yaw in degrees (0 = facing right, -90 = toward the viewer, 90 = away), 40 px",
         labels, cols, make, zoom=3, pool=pool)


def sheet_D(pool):
    cols = ["dawn", "morning", "noon", "golden", "dusk", "overcast"]
    rows = ["bear boar", "bear sow (cinnamon)", "cougar female", "cougar tom"]
    G = {"bear boar": ("walk", 0.3), "bear sow (cinnamon)": ("graze", 0), "cougar female": ("stand", 0), "cougar tom": ("stalk", 0.3)}
    grid("D_individual_x_hour.png", "D. light and hour (48 px; golden and dusk are back-lit: the sun is behind the animal)",
         rows, cols, lambda r, c: (r, *G[r], 48, -18, 8, c, "summer", 2), zoom=3, pool=pool)


def sheet_E(pool):
    cols = ["spring", "summer", "autumn", "winter"]
    rows = ["bear boar", "bear sow", "bear sow (cinnamon)", "bear cub (4 mo)", "bear yearling", "cougar tom", "cougar female", "cougar kitten (3 mo)", "cougar subadult"]

    def make(r, c):
        if "bear" in r and c == "winter":
            return None                  # in the den
        if "cub" in r and c in ("autumn",):
            return (r, "walk", 0.3, 30, -14, 6, "morning", c, 6)
        g = "stand" if "bear" in r else "walk"
        L = 48 if ("cub" not in r and "kitten" not in r) else 30
        return (r, g, 0.25, L, -14, 6, "morning", c, 6)
    grid("E_colour_season_age.png", "E. colour by season and age (bears den Nov/Dec-Mar/Apr: no winter cell; autumn bears ~30% heavier; kittens spotted to ~6 months)",
         rows, cols, make, zoom=3, pool=pool)


def sheet_F(pool):
    cols = [f"{p:.3f}" for p in np.arange(8) / 8]
    rows = [("bear sow", "walk"), ("bear sow", "pace"), ("bear boar", "run"), ("cougar female", "walk"), ("cougar female", "stalk"), ("cougar tom", "run"), ("cougar female", "pounce")]
    labels = [f"{s} {g}" for s, g in rows]

    def make(r, c):
        s, g = rows[labels.index(r)]
        ph = float(c)
        if g == "pounce":
            ph = ph / 0.875
        return (s, g, ph, 48, 0, 6, "morning", "summer", 4)
    grid("F_gait_cycles.png", "F. gait cycles in 8 phases (bear: lateral walk, pace, rotary gallop, after Shine 2015 and the SICB black-bear abstract; cougar: walk, stalk, gallop, pounce after Muybridge's felids and Seton)",
         labels, cols, make, zoom=3, pool=pool)

def deer_group(kind="doe_twins", seed=0, heading=0.0, season="autumn"):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    mem = []
    if kind == "doe_twins":
        hd = np.radians(heading)
        for i, age in enumerate(["adult", "young", "young"]):
            d = -i * rng.uniform(1.6, 2.4)
            mem.append(Member(rig.Individual("deer", "f", age, "summer", seed * 10 + i), "walk", rng.random(),
                              d * np.cos(hd) + rng.normal(0, 0.3), d * np.sin(hd) + rng.normal(0, 0.3), heading + rng.normal(0, 10)))
    elif kind == "buck":
        mem.append(Member(rig.Individual("deer", "m", "adult", season, seed), "alert", 0.0, 0.0, 0.0, heading))
    elif kind == "flee":
        for i in range(3):
            mem.append(Member(rig.Individual("deer", "f" if i else "m", "adult", season, seed * 10 + i), "bound", rng.random(),
                              -i * 3.0 + rng.normal(0, 0.5), rng.normal(0, 1.5), heading + rng.normal(0, 8)))
    return mem


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- compositor

def render_member(args):
    m, k, elev, sun_az, sun_el, hour, depth, ground = args
    import paint, palette as P, render
    from render import View
    render.SHADOW_REACH = 3.0
    # the individual is painted at its own size in px: body length = 1.62 H k
    size = 1.62 * m.ind.spec().H * k
    R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(m.ind, m.gait, m.phase, View(yaw=m.heading, elev=elev, sun_az=sun_az - 0.0, sun_el=sun_el), size_px=size)
    tab = P.palette(m.ind, hour, depth, ground)
    on = R.mat >= 0
    rgb = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    rgb[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
    return rgb, on, R.ground_shadow > 0.45, R.origin


def scene(members, k, W, H, elev=30.0, hour="morning", sun_az=-150.0, sun_el=None, ground="summer", depth=0.0,
          centre=None, seed=0, pool=None):
    """Paint the members on a meadow W x H px at k px/m. Returns RGB."""
    import palette as P, ground as G
    hr = P.HOURS[hour]
    sun_el = sun_el if sun_el is not None else hr["el"]
    e = np.radians(elev)
    if centre is None:
        xs = [m.x for m in members]; ys = [m.y for m in members]
        centre = ((min(xs) + max(xs)) / 2, (min(ys) + max(ys)) / 2)
    tab = P.palette(rig.Individual(), hour, depth,
Write: elements/bear-cougar/src/family.py
"""Families: a sow with cubs, a female cougar with kittens; arrangements from the naturalist's notes
and Seton, and the compositor (shadows first, bodies far to near, true relative scale).

Numbers:
  bear   litter 1-3 (2 commonest); cubs out of the den in April at ~2-5 kg, ~0.34 H of the sow by
         shoulder in May; they keep within a few metres of the sow, climb a tree when alarmed while
         the sow waits at the base, and play (Seton 1926: "Seeing the world with mother").
  cougar kittens 1-4, spotted and ring-tailed to ~6 months, following the female in file or piled
         near her at a kill; the kill cached under raked duff, the family bedding 50-200 m off.
"""
import numpy as np
from dataclasses import dataclass
import rig


@dataclass
class Member:
    ind: object
    gait: str
    phase: float
    x: float           # metres, world
    y: float           # metres, depth (away from the viewer +)
    heading: float     # yaw in degrees (0 = facing screen right)


def bear_family(kind="graze", n_cubs=2, seed=0, heading=0.0, months=4, season="summer", colour="black"):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    sow = rig.Individual("bear", "f", "adult", season, seed, phase_colour=colour)
    mem = []
    hd = np.radians(heading)
    fwd = np.array([np.cos(hd), np.sin(hd)])
    side = np.array([-fwd[1], fwd[0]])
    cubs = [rig.Individual("bear", "m" if i % 2 else "f", "young", season, seed * 10 + i + 1, months=months,
                           phase_colour=("cinnamon" if (colour != "black" or rng.random() < 0.08) else "black")) for i in range(n_cubs)]
    if kind == "graze":
        mem.append(Member(sow, "graze", 0.0, 0.0, 0.0, heading))
        acts = ["sniff", "stand_up", "sit", "walk"]
        for i, c in enumerate(cubs):
            p = rng.uniform(-2.5, 2.0) * fwd + rng.choice([-1, 1]) * rng.uniform(0.9, 2.6) * side
            mem.append(Member(c, acts[i % len(acts)], rng.random(), p[0], p[1], heading + rng.normal(0, 40)))
    elif kind == "file":
        mem.append(Member(sow, "walk", 0.1, 0.0, 0.0, heading))
        for i, c in enumerate(cubs):
            d = -(1.9 + 1.4 * i) + rng.normal(0, 0.3)
            p = d * fwd + rng.normal(0, 0.35) * side
            mem.append(Member(c, "walk", rng.random(), p[0], p[1], heading + rng.normal(0, 8)))
    elif kind == "treed":
        # the cubs up a trunk, the sow at its foot facing out, alert (reared up to smell)
        mem.append(Member(sow, "stand_up" if rng.random() < 0.5 else "alert", 0.0, -1.2, -0.4, heading + 180 + rng.normal(0, 20)))
        for i, c in enumerate(cubs):
            mem.append(Member(c, "climb", 0.25 + 0.4 * i, 0.9 + 0.5 * i, 0.3 * i, heading + (i * 25)))
    elif kind == "fishing":
        mem.append(Member(sow, "fish", 0.2, 0.0, 0.0, heading))
        for i, c in enumerate(cubs):
            mem.append(Member(c, "sit" if i == 0 else "stand", 0.0, -2.5 - 0.8 * i, 2.0 + 0.6 * i, heading + rng.normal(0, 30)))
    return mem


def cougar_family(kind="follow", n_kits=3, seed=0, heading=0.0, months=3, season="summer"):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    fem = rig.Individual("cougar", "f", "adult", season, seed)
    kits = [rig.Individual("cougar", "m" if i % 2 else "f", "young", season, seed * 10 + i + 1, months=months) for i in range(n_kits)]
    hd = np.radians(heading)
    fwd = np.array([np.cos(hd), np.sin(hd)])
    side = np.array([-fwd[1], fwd[0]])
    mem = []
    if kind == "follow":
        mem.append(Member(fem, "walk", 0.1, 0.0, 0.0, heading))
        for i, c in enumerate(kits):
            d = -(1.8 + 1.1 * i) + rng.normal(0, 0.3)
            p = d * fwd + rng.normal(0, 0.4) * side
            mem.append(Member(c, "walk" if rng.random() < 0.7 else "trot", rng.random(), p[0], p[1], heading + rng.normal(0, 12)))
    elif kind == "rest":
        mem.append(Member(fem, "lie", 0.0, 0.0, 0.0, heading))
        acts = [("crouch", 0.0), ("pounce", 0.5), ("sit", 0.0), ("lie", 0.0)]
        for i, c in enumerate(kits):
            g, ph = acts[i % len(acts)]
            p = rng.uniform(-1.8, 1.2) * fwd + rng.choice([-1, 1]) * rng.uniform(0.6, 1.6) * side
            mem.append(Member(c, g, ph, p[0], p[1], heading + rng.normal(0, 60)))
    return mem


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- compositor

def render_member(args):
    m, k, elev, sun_az, sun_el, hour, depth, ground = args
    import paint, palette as P, render
    from render import View
    render.SHADOW_REACH = 2.0
    R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(m.ind, m.gait, m.phase, View(yaw=m.heading, elev=elev, sun_az=sun_az, sun_el=sun_el), k=k)
    tab = P.palette(m.ind, hour, depth, ground)
    on = R.mat >= 0
    rgb = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    rgb[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
    return rgb, on, R.ground_shadow > 0.45, R.origin


def scene(members, k, W, H, elev=30.0, hour="morning", sun_az=-150.0, sun_el=None, ground="summer", depth=0.0,
          centre=None, seed=0, pool=None, bg=None):
    import palette as P, ground as G
    hr = P.HOURS[hour]
    sun_el = sun_el if sun_el is not None else hr["el"]
    e = np.radians(elev)
    if centre is None:
        xs = [m.x for m in members]; ys = [m.y for m in members]
        centre = ((min(xs) + max(xs)) / 2, (min(ys) + max(ys)) / 2)
    tab = P.palette(rig.Individual(), hour, depth, ground)
    if bg is None:
        g = G.meadow((H, W), k, seed=seed, elev=elev)
        img = tab[P.GROUND][g]
        gsh = tab[P.GSHADOW][np.clip(g, 0, 5)]
    else:
        img = bg.copy()
        gsh = (bg.astype(float) * 0.62).astype(np.uint8)
    order = sorted(members, key=lambda m: -m.y)
    jobs = [(m, k, elev, sun_az, sun_el, hour, depth, ground) for m in order]
    res = pool.map(render_member, jobs) if pool else [render_member(j) for j in jobs]

    def place(m):
        return (m.x - centre[0]) * k + W / 2, H / 2 - (m.y - centre[1]) * np.sin(e) * k

    for m, (rgb, on, sh, (ox, oy)) in zip(order, res):
        sx, sy = place(m)
        x0, y0 = int(round(sx - ox)), int(round(sy - oy))
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(sh)
        ys2, xs2 = ys + y0, xs + x0
        ok = (ys2 >= 0) & (ys2 < H) & (xs2 >= 0) & (xs2 < W)
        img[ys2[ok], xs2[ok]] = gsh[ys2[ok], xs2[ok]]
    for m, (rgb, on, sh, (ox, oy)) in zip(order, res):
        sx, sy = place(m)
        x0, y0 = int(round(sx - ox)), int(round(sy - oy))
        ys, xs = np.nonzero(on)
        ys2, xs2 = ys + y0, xs + x0
        ok = (ys2 >= 0) & (ys2 < H) & (xs2 >= 0) & (xs2 < W)
        img[ys2[ok], xs2[ok]] = rgb[ys[ok], xs[ok]]
    return img


def sheet(pool, out="../sheets/G_families.png"):
    from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
    from sheets import _font
    panels = []
    specs = [
        ("black bear sow with two cubs (4 months), grazing: landscape 8 deg, 24 px/m (~12 m)", lambda: bear_family("graze", 2, seed=3, heading=-10), 24, 8, 300, 120),
        ("sow and cubs in file, iso 30, 16 px/m", lambda: bear_family("file", 2, seed=5, heading=15), 16, 30, 300, 120),
        ("cubs treed, sow at the base, landscape 8 deg, 20 px/m", lambda: bear_family("treed", 2, seed=2, heading=0), 20, 8, 240, 170),
        ("sow and 3 cubs grazing, iso 30, 6 px/m (~60 m)", lambda: bear_family("graze", 3, seed=7, heading=20), 6, 30, 160, 80),
        ("cougar female with three kittens (3 months), following: landscape 8 deg, 24 px/m", lambda: cougar_family("follow", 3, seed=1, heading=0), 24, 8, 340, 110),
        ("female resting, kittens playing: iso 30, 24 px/m", lambda: cougar_family("rest", 3, seed=4, heading=-20), 24, 30, 300, 130),
        ("female and kittens following, iso 30, 6 px/m", lambda: cougar_family("follow", 3, seed=2, heading=10), 6, 30, 160, 70),
    ]
    for lab, mk, k, elev, W, H in specs:
        panels.append((lab, scene(mk(), k, W, H, elev=elev, hour="morning", seed=3, pool=pool)))
    Z = 3
    f = _font(12)
    W = max(p.shape[1] for _, p in panels) * Z + 20
    H = sum(p.shape[0] * Z + 28 for _, p in panels) + 40
    im = Image.new("RGB", (W, H), (24, 24, 28))
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    d.text((8, 8), "G. families at true relative scale (cubs and kittens sized by months; a cub of 4 months is ~0.4 of the sow's shoulder height)", fill=(236, 236, 236), font=f)
    y = 34
    for lab, p in panels:
        d.text((10, y), lab, fill=(190, 190, 200), font=f)
        im.paste(Image.fromarray(p.repeat(Z, 0).repeat(Z, 1)), (10, y + 18))
        y += p.shape[0] * Z + 28
    im.save(out)
    print("G_families.png", im.size)

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/bear-cougar/refs; cat > modal_dl.py <<'EOF'
"""Fetch Commons thumbnails from a Modal container (a different IP than the laptop, which Wikimedia
throttles). Returns a tar of the images + sources.tsv.
   modal run modal_dl.py --titles bear_sel.txt --out commons_bear"""
import modal, io, tarfile, os
app = modal.App("voxsim-bear-cougar-refs")
image = modal.Image.debian_slim(python_version="3.12")

@app.function(image=image, timeout=3000, cpu=1)
def fetch(titles):
    import json, urllib.request, urllib.parse, time, re
    UA = "pixelart-studies-refs/1.0 (research; bear-cougar study)"
    def get(u, tries=5):
        for t in range(tries):
            try:
                return urllib.request.urlopen(urllib.request.Request(u, headers={"User-Agent": UA}), timeout=60).read()
            except Exception as e:
                time.sleep(8 * (t + 1))
        return None
    buf = io.BytesIO(); tf = tarfile.open(fileobj=buf, mode="w")
    rows = []
    for i in range(0, len(titles), 40):
        batch = titles[i:i + 40]
        q = urllib.parse.urlencode({"action": "query", "titles": "|".join(batch), "prop": "imageinfo", "iiprop": "url|extmetadata", "iiurlwidth": 960, "format": "json"})
        d = get("https://commons.wikimedia.org/w/api.php?" + q)
        if not d: continue
        for p in json.loads(d)["query"]["pages"].values():
            ii = p.get("imageinfo", [{}])[0]; u = ii.get("thumburl")
            if not u: continue
            safe = re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9]+", "_", p["title"][5:])[:70] + ".jpg"
            b = get(u)
            if b:
                ti = tarfile.TarInfo(safe); ti.size = len(b); tf.addfile(ti, io.BytesIO(b))
                m = ii.get("extmetadata", {}); strip = lambda s: re.sub("<[^>]+>", "", s or "").strip()
                rows.append("\t".join([safe, p["title"], strip(m.get("LicenseShortName", {}).get("value")), strip(m.get("Artist", {}).get("value"))[:80], ii.get("descriptionurl", "")]))
            time.sleep(1.0)
    s = ("\n".join(rows) + "\n").encode()
    ti = tarfile.TarInfo("sources.tsv"); ti.size = len(s); tf.addfile(ti, io.BytesIO(s))
    tf.close()
    return buf.getvalue()

@app.local_entrypoint()
def main(titles: str, out: str):
    ts = [l.strip() for l in open(titles) if l.strip().startswith("File:") and not l.strip().lower().endswith((".webm", ".ogv", ".pdf", ".tif", ".tiff"))]
    data = fetch.remote(ts)
    os.makedirs(out, exist_ok=True)
    tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(data)).extractall(out)
    print("got", len(os.listdir(out)))
EOF

"""Gait GIFs.   python3 anim.py [which ...]
Each frame is one paint_index call at the gait's phase; the animal walks across a still meadow
(the canvas scrolls with it so the feet plant: the ground is repainted per frame at the offset the
body has travelled)."""
import os, sys
os.environ.setdefault("OMP_NUM_THREADS", "1")
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from multiprocessing import Pool
import rig, paint
from render import View

OUT = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "..", "sheets", "anim")

JOBS = {
    "bear_walk_48": (dict(species="bear", sex="m", age="adult"), "walk", 48, 12, 0.0),
    "bear_pace_48": (dict(species="bear", sex="m", age="adult"), "pace", 48, 12, 0.0),
    "bear_run_48": (dict(species="bear", sex="m", age="adult"), "run", 48, 10, 0.0),
    "bear_climb_40": (dict(species="bear", sex="f", age="adult"), "climb", 40, 10, 0.0),
    "cougar_walk_48": (dict(species="cougar", sex="f", age="adult"), "walk", 48, 12, 0.0),
    "cougar_stalk_48": (dict(species="cougar", sex="m", age="adult"), "stalk", 48, 12, 0.0),
    "cougar_run_48": (dict(species="cougar", sex="f", age="adult"), "run", 48, 10, 0.0),
    "cougar_pounce_48": (dict(species="cougar", sex="f", age="adult"), "pounce", 48, 10, 0.0),
    "bear_walk_iso_16": (dict(species="bear", sex="m", age="adult"), "walk", 16, 12, 30.0),
    "cougar_walk_iso_16": (dict(species="cougar", sex="f", age="adult"), "walk", 16, 12, 30.0),
}


def frame(a):
    kw, g, L, ph, elev = a
    ind = rig.Individual(season="summer", seed=2, **kw)
    R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, g, ph, View(yaw=-8 if elev == 0 else -30, elev=6 if elev == 0 else elev, sun_az=-150, sun_el=35), size_px=L)
    return paint.colour(R, ind, "morning", rng=np.random.default_rng(1))


def run(name, pool):
    kw, g, L, n, elev = JOBS[name]
    frames = pool.map(frame, [(kw, g, L, i / n, elev) for i in range(n)])
    H = max(f.shape[0] for f in frames)
    W = max(f.shape[1] for f in frames)
    Z = 3 if L >= 30 else 6
    ims = []
    for f in frames:
        c = np.zeros((H, W, 3), np.uint8)
        c[:] = f[0, 0]
        c[H - f.shape[0]:, :f.shape[1]] = f
        ims.append(Image.fromarray(c.repeat(Z, 0).repeat(Z, 1)))
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    dur = {"walk": 110, "stalk": 170, "pace": 80, "run": 60, "pounce": 70, "climb": 90}.get(g, 100)
    ims[0].save(os.path.join(OUT, name + ".gif"), save_all=True, append_images=ims[1:], duration=dur, loop=0)
    print(name, flush=True)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    which = sys.argv[1:] or list(JOBS)
    with Pool(4) as p:
        for w in which:
            run(w, p)

cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/bear-cougar; cat >> notes/notebook.md <<'EOF'

## 5. Colour and the size ladder (14:00-14:30)

- Bear too grey at every size: the ramp pulled the darks 30% toward the sky's ambient colour, which lifts black to ~40/255. Black fur: ambient pull cut to a quarter (the sky tints only the sheen), the value field compressed (x = 0.15 + 0.74 x) and the sheen family made steeper; no hair dither on black fur except in the sheen band. Result: ramp 13 -> 74.
- Cougar colour from ODFW's Oregon cat (Commons): rufous tawny, cream underside only as a thin edge. The pale leg patches were the "back" of the leg tubes (their up-normal is forward): the inner leg is the lateral normal.
- Glyph painter (micro.py): first cougar glyph was a hollow tent in mid-tawny steps 2-3, the same value as the grass. Lit tawny on top (4), 3 below, pale belly row. Found a front/back flip: the lofted torso runs rear -> front (loft sorts by x), so tor.c[0] is the RUMP.
- Poses: climb needed paw targets in body coordinates ("ikb"), because a pitched body's roots move; sit geometry printed and was right for the bear, wrong for the cat (the tibia went up: -150 deg); pounce rebuilt as three keyframes (launch, flight, strike).

## 6. Critic round 1 (cold subagent), 14:35

Bear reads at every size ("a black lump" at 12-6 px, protect it); the cougar fails from mid size down ("a tan fox or fawn"). Ranked:

1. The tail runs as a rope to the ground with the black tip lying flat "like a hoof". Real: lowest point ~0.15-0.2 H up, last ~15% curling up.
2. Cougar legs spindly: Seton draws thick forearms and paws wider than the wrist.
3. Ears read as posts/horns: short, rounded, set wide, dark only on the rear edge.
4. Hard seams at shoulder and hip ("armour plates"): the internal masses' terminators.
5. Bear back two blobs (the scapula tube bulged above the back): one dome, rump highest.
6. Too much daylight under the bear.
7. Cougar body an even tube.
Poses failing: rearing bear "a man in a black coat" (bolt upright, no belly); lying bear "a rug"; climbing bear "a bag stuck to a capsule"; salmon a speck; pace = walk; cougar run a rocking horse; pounce flight an otter; cat sit too tall; trot = walk.

Fixes: tail formula rebuilt and tuned by printing its lowest point and tip (stand: min 0.21 H, tip 0.31); forearm/paw radii up ~30%; ears 0.07 H; internal masses (shoulder, upper limb, flank fold) may not draw terminators; bear scapula top down to 0.84 H and belly to 0.30 H; rearing bear slouched at 64 deg with the belly out and the forepaws limp in front; salmon 0.85 H long; pounce flight forelegs forward-down.
EOF

my composition (a wide channel, one rib) is far from the photo's narrow rock chute.

- *Fall vs Deception Falls*: **not close.** A cascade is ribbons winding among mossy boulders; mine
  was a rectangular curtain on terraced walls. The cascade style now uses the boulder-cascade
  regime of the rapid site (the solver finds the ribbons); the plunge face is still too flat.

**Stage 4: refinement of the rapid at scene scale** (`out/s4_rapid_low_scene_golden_3x.png`): the
cobble bar via the cobbles study's ladder (mass first, one tick per stone at 2-5 px, headroom so
marks go both ways), coherent rough/smooth boundaries, despeckled marks.

**Stage 5: matching the photos, with critics and an answer key.** After the coordinator's note
("match the reference, first its form, then its detail") I built two instruments and started
calling cold critics on every photo | mine pair (`CRITIQUE.md`, rounds 1-4):

- `src/measure.py`: both crops at the same native size; notan (3 value groups), L\* histogram,
  stroke-direction histogram, busyness per value group, Lab per group, histogram distance.
- `src/repaint.py`: segment the downsampled photo's own water, synthesize a G-buffer (depth from
  distance to shore, the photo's own shadow), repaint only the water with my rules, and compare the
  water region directly. On Colchuck: lit water photo L\* 60.7 a -29.7 | mine 65.9 a -34.2; shaded water
  photo 23.8 b -18.6 | mine 27.7 b -13.5 (after the reflectance-ladder mirror; it was 33.8 before).
- Willow's warning held for me too: the histogram distance improved on the tidal current from 11.9
  to 2.3 while the picture stayed wrong until the critic named the drawn-contour foam lines. The
  measures are instruments; the critics and my eyes judge.

What changed, per type (before/after pairs in `out/cmp_*_v*.png`):

Write: elements/bear-cougar/src/context.py
"""Animals put into other students' paintings.   python3 context.py [rapid] [ledge] [dusk]

rapid  black bears at the water student's Skykomish rapid, golden hour (water/out/
       s4_rapid_low_scene_golden_3x.png, native 320 x 200 at 13 px/m, camera 30 deg; sun from the
       front-left, 14 deg up: the water student's golden hour is sun_az 250 clockwise from the
       camera's forward, which is -160 in this study's convention).
dusk   a bear carrying a salmon up the channel student's dusk gravel bar (channel/out/
       s5_low_dusk_4x.png, the elk student's context painting), back-lit.
ledge  a cougar on a ledge above blacktail deer (the deer painted by the elk student's own code).
"""
import os, sys, subprocess, json
os.environ.setdefault("OMP_NUM_THREADS", "1")
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint, render, palette as P
from render import View

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
EL = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "..")
OUT = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "stages", "context")


def sprite(ind, gait, phase, k, yaw, elev, sun_az, sun_el, hour, depth=0.0, shadow_reach=1.0, pose=None):
    render.SHADOW_REACH = shadow_reach
    R, tubes, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, gait, phase, View(yaw=yaw, elev=elev, sun_az=sun_az, sun_el=sun_el), k=k, pose=pose)
    tab = P.palette(ind, hour, depth)
    on = R.mat >= 0
    rgb = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8)
    rgb[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
    return rgb, on, R.ground_shadow > 0.45, R.origin


def paste(img, spr, at, shadow_tint=(40, 44, 70), shadow_mix=0.45, water=None):
    rgb, on, sh, (ox, oy) = spr
    H, W, _ = img.shape
    x0, y0 = int(round(at[0] - ox)), int(round(at[1] - oy))
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(sh & ~on)
    ys2, xs2 = ys + y0, xs + x0
    ok = (ys2 >= 0) & (ys2 < H) & (xs2 >= 0) & (xs2 < W)
    if water is not None:
        ok &= ~water[np.clip(ys2, 0, H - 1), np.clip(xs2, 0, W - 1)]
    c = img[ys2[ok], xs2[ok]].astype(float)
    img[ys2[ok], xs2[ok]] = (c * (1 - shadow_mix) * 0.75 + np.array(shadow_tint) * shadow_mix * 0.75 + c * 0.25 * 0).clip(0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
    ys, xs = np.nonzero(on)
    ys2, xs2 = ys + y0, xs + x0
    ok = (ys2 >= 0) & (ys2 < H) & (xs2 >= 0) & (xs2 < W)
    img[ys2[ok], xs2[ok]] = rgb[ys[ok], xs[ok]]
    return img


def splash(img, pts, cols):
    for (x, y), c in zip(pts, cols):
        if 0 <= y < img.shape[0] and 0 <= x < img.shape[1]:
            img[y, x] = c
    return img


def rapid():
    src = os.path.join(EL, "water", "out", "s4_rapid_low_scene_golden_3x.png")
    big = Image.open(src).convert("RGB")
    img = np.array(big.resize((big.width // 3, big.height // 3), Image.NEAREST))
    k = 13.0
    az, el, hour = -160.0, 14.0, "golden"
    # water mask: the teal and the foam (no cast shadow on moving water)
    f = img.astype(int)
    water = (f[..., 1] > f[..., 0] + 8) | ((f[..., 0] > 170) & (f[..., 2] > 150) & (np.abs(f[..., 0] - f[..., 2]) < 40))
    # 1. a boar on the big boulder's top, facing the white-water sill, a forepaw raised to pin
    boar = rig.Individual("bear", "m", "adult", "autumn", 4)
    img = paste(img, sprite(boar, "fish", 0.2, k, 198, 30, az, el, hour), (231, 95), water=None)
    # a chum salmon jumping at the sill below him: a 4-px silver-and-blush arc
    img = splash(img, [(212, 110), (213, 109), (214, 109), (215, 110), (216, 111)],
                 [(214, 218, 212), (238, 236, 226), (196, 150, 150), (170, 120, 124), (230, 232, 226)])
    # 2. a sow carrying a salmon up the cobble bar toward the forest, a cub of the year behind her
    sow = rig.Individual("bear", "f", "adult", "autumn", 7)
    img = paste(img, sprite(sow, "carry", 0.3, k, -55, 30, az, el, hour), (281, 132), water=water)
    cub = rig.Individual("bear", "f", "young", "autumn", 71, months=9)
    img = paste(img, sprite(cub, "walk", 0.7, k, -40, 30, az, el, hour), (268, 150), water=water)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    Image.fromarray(img).save(os.path.join(OUT, "rapid_bears_1x.png"))
    Image.fromarray(img.repeat(4, 0).repeat(4, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "rapid_bears_4x.png"))
    c = img[70:170, 170:320]
    Image.fromarray(c.repeat(8, 0).repeat(8, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "rapid_bears_crop_8x.png"))
    print("rapid ok")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    for w in (sys.argv[1:] or ["rapid"]):
        globals()[w]()

"""Renderer vs glyph painter at 14..4 px, side by side, to choose the handoff.  python3 handoff.py TAG"""
import sys, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint
from render import View
SUB=[("bear","m","walk",0.25),("bear","f","graze",0),("cougar","f","walk",0.25),("cougar","m","stalk",0.3),("cougar","f","stand",0)]
rows=[]
for view in ((-12,6),(-35,30)):
  for spn,sex,g,ph in SUB:
    cells=[]
    for L in (14,12,10,8,7,6,5,4):
        for micro in (99,0):
            paint.MICRO=micro
            ind=rig.Individual(spn,sex,"adult","summer",3)
            R,_,_=paint.paint_index(ind,g,ph,View(yaw=view[0],elev=view[1],sun_az=-150,sun_el=35),size_px=L)
            cells.append(paint.colour(R,ind,"morning",rng=np.random.default_rng(1)))
    rows.append(cells)
hh=[max(c.shape[0] for c in r) for r in rows]; n=len(rows[0])
cw=[max(r[i].shape[1] for r in rows) for i in range(n)]
out=np.full((sum(hh)+3*len(rows), sum(cw)+2*n+ (n//2)*4,3),20,np.uint8); y=0
for r,h in zip(rows,hh):
    x=0
    for i,c in enumerate(r):
        out[y+h-c.shape[0]:y+h,x:x+c.shape[1]]=c; x+=cw[i]+2+(4 if i%2 else 0)
    y+=h+3
Image.fromarray(out.repeat(6,0).repeat(6,1)).save('../stages/s2/handoff_'+sys.argv[1]+'.png'); print(out.shape)
EOF

Bash: Head close-up test, cropped
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/bear-cougar/src; cat > headtest.py <<'EOF'
import sys, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import rig, render, paint, hide
from render import View
out=[]
for spn,g in (("cougar","alert"),("cougar","walk"),("bear","stand")):
    for yaw in (0,-40,-90):
        ind=rig.Individual(spn,"f","adult","summer",1); sp=ind.spec()
        pose=rig.gait_pose(sp,g,0.25)
        tubes,_=rig.build(ind,pose)
        keep=[t for t in tubes if t.name.startswith(("head","neck","ear","jaw","torso","shoulder","LF","RF"))]
        k=34/(sp.head_len*sp.H)
        v=View(yaw=yaw,elev=6,sun_az=-150,sun_el=35,ss=3)
        R=render.rasterize(keep,ind,v,k,pad=3)
        R=render.thin_lines(R,keep,ind,v,k)
        on=(R.cov>=0.45)|R.thin; R.mat=np.where(on,R.mat,-1)
        x,far=paint.value_field(R,v,keep,ind); R.x=x; R.far=far
        R=hide.apply(R,v,keep,60,flatten=0.1,dark_mats=[],species=spn)
        img=paint.colour(R,ind,"morning",bg=np.full(R.mat.shape+(3,),200,np.uint8))
        names=[t.name for t in keep]
        hp=np.isin(R.part,[i for i,n in enumerate(names) if n.startswith(("head","ear","jaw"))])
        ys,xs=np.nonzero(hp)
        if len(ys)==0: continue
        cy,cx=int(ys.mean()),int(xs.mean())
        c=img[max(0,cy-30):cy+30,max(0,cx-34):cx+34]
        out.append(c)
H=max(i.shape[0] for i in out); W=sum(i.shape[1]+6 for i in out)
S=np.full((H,W,3),200,np.uint8); x=0
for i in out: S[:i.shape[0],x:x+i.shape[1]]=i; x+=i.shape[1]+6
Image.fromarray(S).resize((W*5,H*5),Image.NEAREST).save('../stages/s2/heads_'+sys.argv[1]+'.png')
EOF
nice python3 headtest.py h2

Write: elements/bear-cougar/src/valboard.py
"""The value board (the instrument the elk student wished it had built): photo area-reduced to the
hero size | mine at the same size, both as luminance quantised to 6 steps over the animal's own
range, compared plane by plane.   python3 valboard.py TAG [size]"""
import sys, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import rig, paint
from render import View
from board import entries


def lum(a):
    a = a.astype(float) / 255
    return 0.2126 * a[..., 0] + 0.7152 * a[..., 1] + 0.0722 * a[..., 2]


def quant(L, mask, n=6):
    v = L[mask]
    lo, hi = np.percentile(v, 3), np.percentile(v, 97)
    q = np.clip(((L - lo) / max(hi - lo, 1e-3) * n).astype(int), 0, n - 1)
    out = (q / (n - 1) * 200 + 30).astype(np.uint8)
    out[~mask] = 255
    return out


def row(e, Lpx):
    f, crop, gr, wr, (spn, sex), g, ph, yaw, lab = e[:9]
    elev = e[9] if len(e) > 9 else 0
    im = Image.open(f).convert("RGB").crop(crop)
    ind = rig.Individual(spn, sex, "adult", "summer", 1)
    sp = ind.spec()
    hbH = paint.hb_m(ind) / sp.H
    s = (Lpx / hbH) / (gr - wr)
    p = np.array(im.resize((max(1, int(im.width * s)), max(1, int(im.height * s))), Image.BOX))
    R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, g, ph, View(yaw=yaw, elev=max(elev, 4), sun_el=45, sun_az=-145), size_px=Lpx)
    mine = paint.colour(R, ind, "morning", rng=np.random.default_rng(2))
    on = R.mat >= 0
    # the photo's animal mask: the darkest / most saturated blob is too fragile to segment; use the
    # whole crop's luminance but quantise over the central band (the animal fills it)
    Lp = lum(p)
    pm = np.ones(Lp.shape, bool)
    qp = quant(Lp, pm)
    qm = quant(lum(mine), on)
    hh = max(p.shape[0], mine.shape[0])
    out = np.full((hh, p.shape[1] * 2 + mine.shape[1] * 2 + 18, 3), 255, np.uint8)
    x = 0
    for a in (p, np.stack([qp] * 3, -1), mine, np.stack([qm] * 3, -1)):
        out[hh - a.shape[0]:, x:x + a.shape[1]] = a
        x += a.shape[1] + 6
    return out


if __name__ == "__main__":
    tag = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "a"
    L = int(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else 60
    rows = [row(e, L) for e in entries()]
    W = max(r.shape[1] for r in rows)
    out = np.full((sum(r.shape[0] + 4 for r in rows), W, 3), 255, np.uint8)
    y = 0
    for r in rows:
        out[y:y + r.shape[0], :r.shape[1]] = r
        y += r.shape[0] + 4
    Image.fromarray(out.repeat(4, 0).repeat(4, 1)).save(f"../stages/s1/values_{tag}.png")
    print("ok")

## 7. Muybridge at last, and the rest of the sheets (14:50-15:30)

- Commons rate-limited the laptop's IP all afternoon (429 on every media request). Fetched the plates from a Modal container instead (`refs/modal_dl.py`, app `voxsim-bear-cougar-refs`, 1 CPU, a few minutes): plates 716-731, 744-745 in `refs/muybridge/`.
- Plate 720 (cat galloping, 24 frames) against my run: my cat was stilted, its stride splayed, its tail a horizontal stick. Muybridge's cat stays LOW through the whole gallop, legs bunched under an arched back in the gathered frames, and the tail rides UP and back. Stride 1.15 -> 0.95 H, lifts 0.30 -> 0.22, body 0.07 H lower, tail root +8 deg with no droop.
- Plate 721 (lion walking): head carried at or a little below the withers line, forearm vertical at mid-stance, hind foot landing in the fore print. Matches the walk table.
- Value board (valboard.py, the instrument the elk student named as its next one): photo and mine at 60 px quantised to six steps over their own range. The bear photo is ONE value with a sheen on the back only; mine had four steps of lit planes. Compressed the bear's field again (x = 0.1 + 0.62 x). The cougar drawings carry a shadowed belly and legs; my cream belly glowed like a lit plane: the belly's dark end now falls to shadow.
- Handoff study (handoff.py): renderer vs glyph at 14-4 px. Bear: the glyph is the cleaner lump from 10 px down. Cougar: the renderer keeps mass and the tail's curl down to 8 px, the glyph is a stick figure above that. Per-species handoff: bear 10, cougar 8. Cougar glyph body deepened to 0.52 H.
- Heads (headtest.py): the cougar's face read as a sheep (big pink nose disc, eye far back, a dark "saddle" blob on the cheek from the spine-saddle rule). Shorter muzzle (0.26 of a 0.28 H head), a small nose on the tip, the eye forward at u = 0.6, pale cheek and lip, dark whisker spot; the saddle rule deleted.
- Context 1: black bears on the water student's Skykomish rapid at golden hour (13 px/m, 30 deg): a boar fishing from the big boulder, a sow carrying a salmon up the cobble bar with a cub.
EOF

def dusk_gravel_bar():
    bg = np.array(Image.open(f"{STUDIO}/elements/channel/out/s5_low_dusk_4x.png").convert("RGB"))[::4, ::4].copy()
    H, W, _ = bg.shape
    # water mask: the channel is the light violet-grey band; read it off the image by colour
    b = bg.astype(int)
    water = (b[..., 2] > 150) & (b[..., 0] > 150) & (np.arange(H)[:, None] > 60)
    sky = tuple(int(v) for v in bg[20, W // 2])
    hour, sun_az, sun_el = "dusk", 95.0, 5.0     # the glow is behind them, a little to the right

    def k_at(y):                                  # perspective read off the gravel: px per metre
        return max(0.8, 1.0 + (y - 50) * 0.13)
    cast = []
    rng = np.random.default_rng(3)
    # cows scattered on the far bar, the bull nearer, one cow walking in the shallows
    for i, (x, y) in enumerate([(30, 64), (46, 62), (70, 66), (96, 63), (120, 68), (150, 64), (20, 70), (84, 71)]):
        ind = rig.Individual("elk", "f" if i != 5 else "m", "adult" if i != 3 else "young", "autumn", 40 + i)
        g = ["graze", "graze", "walk", "graze", "alert", "alert", "graze", "walk"][i]
        cast.append((y, ind, g, rng.random(), rng.uniform(-40, 40) + (0 if rng.random() < 0.6 else 180), x))
    cast.append((108, rig.Individual("elk", "m", "adult", "autumn", 7), "alert", 0.0, -15.0, 70))
    cast.append((112, rig.Individual("elk", "f", "adult", "autumn", 8), "walk", 0.35, -160.0, 205))
    img = bg.copy()
    for y, ind, g, ph, yaw, x in sorted(cast, key=lambda c: c[0]):
        k = k_at(y)
        depth = float(np.clip((80 - y) / 40, 0, 0.7))
        rgb, on, sh, org, R = sprite(ind, g, ph, yaw, 6.0, k, hour, sun_az, sun_el, depth=depth, haze=sky)
        paste(img, rgb, on, sh, org, x, y, water=water if water[min(y + 1, H - 1), x] else None)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    Image.fromarray(img).save(os.path.join(OUT, "dusk_gravel_bar_1x.png"))
    Image.fromarray(img.repeat(4, 0).repeat(4, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "dusk_gravel_bar_4x.png"))
    # detail crop around the bull at 8x
    Image.fromarray(img[70:122, 30:120].repeat(8, 0).repeat(8, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "dusk_gravel_bar_bull_8x.png"))


def dot(img, x, y, ind, heading, rng, taken):
    """The dot tier (an elk ~1.3 px long at 0.55 px/m): two pixels, the pale rump behind and the
    dark front toward the head, and a 1-px shadow tick on the shadow side. Calves are a single
    mid-brown pixel. Dots never touch. (Single cream pixels read as daisies or gravel; a dark
    front and pale back, the naturalist's far read, is what says animal.)"""
    H, W, _ = img.shape
    fx = 1 if np.cos(np.radians(heading)) >= 0 else -1
    xs = [x, x + fx]
    if not (1 <= min(xs) and max(xs) < W - 1 and 1 <= y < H - 1) or taken[y - 1:y + 2, min(xs) - 1:max(xs) + 2].any():
        return
    pale = np.array((226, 204, 162))
    dark = np.array((96, 70, 50))
    img[y + 1, x + 1] = darken(img[y + 1, x + 1][None], 0.66)[0]
    if ind.age == "young":
        img[y, x] = np.array((140, 100, 72))

Bash: Render blacktail deer with elk student's code
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/deer-elk/src; cat > /tmp/seton/deer_sprites.py <<'EOF'
import sys, numpy as np
sys.path.insert(0, '.')
import rig, paint, palette as P, render
from render import View
render.SHADOW_REACH = 1.0
out = {}
for i, (sex, age, g, ph, yaw, k) in enumerate([("f", "adult", "graze", 0.0, 20, 22), ("f", "young", "walk", 0.4, 10, 23), ("m", "adult", "alert", 0.0, -160, 21)]):
    ind = rig.Individual("deer", sex, age, "summer", 30 + i)
    size = 1.62 * ind.spec().H * k
    R, _, _ = paint.paint_index(ind, g, ph, View(yaw=yaw, elev=12, sun_az=-150, sun_el=35), size_px=size)
    tab = P.palette(ind, "morning", 0.0, "summer")
    on = R.mat >= 0
    rgb = np.zeros(R.mat.shape + (3,), np.uint8); rgb[on] = tab[R.mat[on], R.step[on]]
    out[f"rgb{i}"] = rgb; out[f"on{i}"] = on; out[f"sh{i}"] = R.ground_shadow > 0.45; out[f"org{i}"] = np.array(R.origin)
np.savez("/tmp/seton/deer_sprites.npz", **out)
print("ok")
EOF
nice python3 /tmp/seton/deer_sprites.py

        img = paste(img, spr, at, shadow_tint=(40, 44, 60), shadow_mix=0.4)
    # the cougar on the pale boulder's crest, crouched, facing the deer, tail laid along the rock
    cat = rig.Individual("cougar", "f", "adult", "summer", 5)
    img = paste(img, sprite(cat, "crouch", 0.0, 19.0, -8, 12, az, el, hour), (186, 160), shadow_tint=(40, 44, 60), shadow_mix=0.4)
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    Image.fromarray(img).save(os.path.join(OUT, "ledge_cougar_1x.png"))
    Image.fromarray(img.repeat(3, 0).repeat(3, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "ledge_cougar_3x.png"))
    Image.fromarray(img[120:250, 130:370].repeat(5, 0).repeat(5, 1)).save(os.path.join(OUT, "ledge_cougar_crop_5x.png"))
    print("ledge ok")


if __name__ == "__main__":''')
open('context.py','w').write(s)

## 8. Context pieces and critic round 2 (15:30-16:10)

- Dusk bar (channel student's painting, the elk student's scale k(y) = 1 + 0.13 (y - 50)): a cougar crossing at ~10 px, a bear leaving the water with a chum. First try the cougar was 6 px, a glyph skeleton; moved nearer. The back-lit bear at 14 px was a black hole: the rim rule lived inside the >= 20 px block; the rim now runs from 10 px up.
- Ledge (rock student's granite boulder field, its own render at native 383 x 287): the cougar crouched on the pale boulder's crest, three blacktails on the talus below painted by the elk student's code as a subprocess (`notes/deer_sprites.py`). First try the deer were twice the cat's size: the deer placed nearer than the cat at a bigger k; moved them back up the talus (k 13 against the cat's 23).
- Critic round 2 (cold): the bear reads at every size; the cougar's VALUE is far too light (near-white value maps; the photos and Seton's cats are mid-value with a darker back and tail); the tail is a thin rope and rides up at 45 deg in the run (Muybridge 720 streams it low); the back-light rim runs round the whole body like a sticker border; cubs and kittens are scaled-down adults; the bear's muzzle hooks like a beak; the walking bear glyph at 5-8 px is "a comb" (four leg stubs); the stalk's head sits level with the shoulders; the ledge cougar is warm orange in a cool grey scene and floats; the dusk bear is a crenellated box.
- Fixes: cougar body a step darker (52,34,24)-(182,130,88) plus a darker spine band (torso only); tail radius 0.034 -> 0.05 H; the run's tail at the back line; the rim only on upward-facing fur edges, never legs; cub heads x1.32 and legs x0.88, kitten heads x1.3 with fewer, bolder spots; the bear's profile straightened; bear glyph draws the far legs only from 9 px; stalk neck -20 and scapulae 0.14 H; crouch head down and tail laid along; the ledge cat and deer take the scene's cool sky as haze (depth 0.12), the deer in their grey autumn coat.
EOF
echo ok

Write: elements/bear-cougar/LESSONS.md
# Black bear and cougar: lessons

A student's log. Subject: the two big predators of Washington's forests, the **black bear** (boar, sow, yearling, cub; black, brown and cinnamon phases) and the **cougar** (tom, female, subadult, spotted kitten), from a 60 px hero animal down to a 3 px mark, in the landscape view (camera 6-8 deg) and the 30 deg iso view, walking, pacing, running, grazing, sniffing, standing up, sitting, lying, fishing, carrying a salmon and climbing (bear); standing, alert, walking, stalking, trotting, galloping, sitting, lying, crouching and pouncing (cougar); at six hours and four seasons; with cubs and kittens beside their mothers.

Everything here is made by the programs in `src/`. Stage images are in `stages/`, parameter sheets and GIFs in `sheets/`, references in `refs/` (licences in each `sources.tsv`), the observation with sources in `notes/observation.md`, the raw working thread in `notes/notebook.md`.



cd src
nice -n 10 python3 sheets.py            # sheets A-H (~25 min on 4 processes on a loaded laptop)
nice -n 10 python3 anim.py              # 10 gait GIFs
nice -n 10 python3 context.py           # rapid, dusk, ledge: the animals in other students&#x27; paintings
python3 board.py notan|grey|colour|sizes TAG ; python3 valboard.py TAG 60   # the instruments




One animal is one call:



import rig, paint
from render import View
ind = rig.Individual(&quot;cougar&quot;, &quot;f&quot;, &quot;adult&quot;, season=&quot;autumn&quot;, seed=2)   # species, sex, age, season, seed
#   bear extras: phase_colour=&quot;black&quot;|&quot;brown&quot;|&quot;cinnamon&quot;; young: months=4
R, tubes, k = paint.paint_index(ind, gait=&quot;stalk&quot;, phase=0.3,          # see the gait list below
                                view=View(yaw=-14, elev=8, sun_az=-150, sun_el=30, diffuse=0),
                                size_px=48)                             # nose to tail root, px
#   or k=... pixels per metre for true relative scale (herds, families, scenes)
rgb = paint.colour(R, ind, hour=&quot;golden&quot;, ground=&quot;autumn&quot;, depth=0.0)  # the palette is the light




Gaits and poses: bear `stand walk pace run graze sniff sit stand_up lie fish carry climb alert`; cougar `stand alert walk stalk trot run sit lie crouch pounce`. `phase` in [0, 1) for cycles (and for `fish` <0.5 paw raised / >=0.5 salmon in the mouth; `pounce` 0 launch, 0.5 flight, 1 strike).

| module | what it is |
|---|---|
| `rig.py` | the animals as 3-D tubes: torsos lofted from measured stations, plantigrade and digitigrade legs, the cougar's J-tail, sex/age/season/seed variation, every pose and gait |
| `render.py`, `hide.py`, `fringe.py`, `ground.py` | forked from the elk student: splat renderer, index canvas, Ferrari edge rules; mine: black-fur quantisation, the bear's shag, the rim only on top fur |
| `coat.py`, `palette.py` | coat regions (muzzle, blaze, cougar underside, spine band, whisker spot, ear backs, tail tip, kitten spots and rings) and ramps per coat and hour |
| `micro.py` | the glyph painter below the handoff (bear 10 px, cougar 8 px) |
| `family.py` | sow with cubs, female with kittens, and the compositor |
| `board.py`, `valboard.py`, `ladder.py`, `handoff.py`, `poses.py`, `headtest.py`, `glyphdbg.py` | the instruments |
| `sheets.py`, `anim.py`, `context.py` | the deliverables |

---

## 1. The model system: Seton, standing where Muybridge didn't

The elk student stood on Muybridge's plates and the naturalist's four things. For these two animals, Muybridge is half a model: his catalogue (UPenn's 1887 prospectus, `refs/lit/muybridge_catalogue.pdf`) has a domestic cat walking, trotting and galloping (plates 716-720), a lion, lioness, tigress and jaguar walking (721-731) and a raccoon (744-745), and **no bear at all**. Nobody's frame-by-frame bear exists before the biomechanics lab.

The system I found and stood on is **Ernest Thompson Seton's**, the naturalist-painter of North American mammals, all of it public domain on archive.org:

- ***Studies in the Art Anatomy of Animals* (1896)**, written "for the use of sculptors, painters, illustrators, naturalists and taxidermists". It measures animals the way figure drawing measures people, in head-lengths: *"The Grizzly, Brown and Black Bears go in a square of two heads ... the arch of the back rises considerably above the line"*; *"A Cat goes in a rectangle of 2 1/2 heads in height by 3 1/2 heads in length"*, and the cougar *"in all its proportions and anatomy agrees with the Cat"*; the tail half the total length; the belly at half the shoulder height. Plate XXVII (the proportions of the lion) gives every limb bone as one head. [`refs/lit/seton_plates/`]
- ***Lives of Game Animals* (1925-26)**: measured sizes (a Colorado sow: 1613 mm snout to tail tip, tail 127, shoulder 648 mm; cougar toms 2287 mm total, females 1982 with 814 mm of tail), colour ("deep glossy black everywhere, excepting the muzzle, which is more or less brown, and the white patch commonly seen on the breast"; the cougar's "dark spot at the base of the whiskers", dark ear backs, "the tip of the tail nearly black"; kittens spotted to ~6 months), behaviour, and his drawings from life: cougars standing, walking, crouched and lying (pl. X-XV), bears sitting like a person, rearing, climbing, and a page of bear silhouettes in pure notan that is exactly what a small figure should be.

I added both, and the felid Muybridge plates, to the studio library README. For gait I stood on the biomechanics: **Shine et al. 2015** (grizzlies walk to 2 m/s, then a *running walk*, then a canter; **no trot**; the forefoot placed medially with a side-to-side waddle) and the SICB black-bear abstract (black bears *pace* at intermediate speed); for the pounce, **Williams et al. 2014** (SMART-collared pumas; pounce force matched to prey, 5-16 g). The observation half for the sim, with every number sourced, is `notes/observation.md`.

## 2. The rig: two kinds of leg

![first render](stages/s1/t1_grey.png)

*The first render. The bear already reads as a bear: a deep barrel on short legs gets you most of the way. The cougar has stilts, a horse's head and a tail pooled on the ground.*

I forked the elk student's renderer, hide rules and palette machinery and wrote a new rig. What's new is the leg. A leg is a root (the shoulder or hip joint) → the upper bone → an IK joint (the elbow bends back, the knee forward) → the lower bone → the wrist or ankle → the metapodial **at a pose-given angle** → the digits. The stance angle of the metapodial *is* the species:

- the bear is **plantigrade**: the hind metapodial lies flat on the ground (84° from vertical), the heel down, the forepaws turned in 18° (pigeon toes); in the walk the heel peels up late in stance and the forepaw flips palm-up in the swing;
- the cougar is **digitigrade**: it stands on its toes, the metatarsus sloping at 28°, the hock high behind; the forearm vertical under the elbow.

The cougar's tail is a 14-station chain: from the croup at a root angle, falling toward vertical by a `droop`, its last 40% curling back and up by a `lift`. I tune it by printing its lowest point and tip height in shoulder heights, not by eye (standing: lowest 0.21 H, tip 0.31 H).

## 3. The board

`board.py` is the elk student's instrument: photo | mine at 150 px shoulder, same ground row, first in notan. The references: the Whistler black bear walking (GoToVan, CC BY), **Seton's cougar standing and walking, drawn from life** (pl. XII, XIII: a drawn reference is as good as a photo on this board, often better, because Seton drew the side view on purpose), and ODFW's walking Oregon cougar (camera ~25° up; the board scales the withers row by 1/cos).

![notan b1](stages/s1/board_notan_b1.png)
![notan b3](stages/s1/board_notan_b3.png)

*b1 and b3, four runs apart. What changed, in order:*

1. **The cougar's legs.** Too short, the forearm slanting forward, because I'd put the paw ahead of the shoulder joint. Printing the joints showed it. Seton's cat stands with its forearm vertical under the elbow and the belly at half the shoulder height (the canon). Torso raised, the croup 0.08 H above the withers (pl. XII shows ~0.1).
2. **The cougar's head.** A horse's: 0.37 H long with a long muzzle. Now 0.28 H, the muzzle a quarter of it, round skull.
3. **The bear's head.** Too long and horizontal, with spike ears. And the walking bear's head was STILL horizontal after I fixed the standing carriage, because the walk table overrode it; the photo carries the nose at ~0.42 H, below the back line.
4. **The bear's belly** down to 0.30 H: the Whistler bear's legs are short columns under a deep barrel.
5. **The cougar's tail**: thinner, swung back; later, a lot more on the tail (§6).

![grey b6](stages/s1/board_grey_b6.png)

*The grey board after those fixes. The cougar's thigh and flank still came out as separate tubes; I added a flank fold (skin from belly to stifle) so the belly line flows into the knee.*

## 4. At size, and the black-fur problem

![sizes b5](stages/s1/sizes_b5.png)

*sizes_b5: photo and drawings area-reduced to 60, 30, 16, 8 px nose to rump | mine. The bear is a GREY bear.*

The elk's palette walks every ramp from an ambient-tinted dark to a sun-tinted light, which lifts the darks 30% toward the sky. On tan hide that's lovely. On a black bear it lifts black to 40/255 and the bear is grey. Worse, the lit family spans three steps, so the flank shows lit planes a black bear doesn't have. The fixes, which together are how I'd paint black fur:

- **the ambient pull on black fur is cut to a quarter**: the sky tints only the sheen, never the darks;
- **the value field is compressed and steepened**: most of the bear sits in the bottom two steps; only surfaces facing up and toward the sun climb into a narrow blue-grey sheen along the back and the top of the head;
- **no dither on black fur** except in the sheen band, where it breaks into hair dashes;
- the one warm mark is the **brown muzzle**, which at 6 px and below becomes one pixel: it's the pixel that says which way the bear faces.

![values](stages/s1/values_v1.png)

*The value board (`valboard.py`, the instrument the elk student named as its next): photo at 60 px, its 6-step value map, mine, mine's map. The bear photo is one value with a sheen on the back only; at this stage mine had four lit planes, and the cougar was near-white, both corrected after this image (final board below).*

## 5. Small: two painters, two handoffs

![ladder l1](stages/s2/ladder_l1.png)

*The first ladder, 60 px to 4 px, landscape and iso. The bears held up at every size; the cougar dissolved below 16 px into a tan tent, the same value as the grass.*

The glyph painter (`micro.py`) follows the elk student's rules (body mass per column, legs as clean runs, a head pixel up or down) with the species' own marks: for the bear, a deep mass (0.62 H in rows), a head lower than the back, round ear ticks, the muzzle pixel, no tail; for the cougar, a shallow mass on long legs, a pale belly row, the croup a row high, and **the tail drawn at every size down to 4 px, its tip an up-tick one row above the tail's lowest pixel**, dark (a flat dark dot on the ground read as a hoof). Printing the glyph as characters found two bugs: the lofted torso runs rear→front (loft sorts by x), so "front" was the rump; and the cougar glyph was drawn in steps 2-3 of tawny, which is the grass's value.

![handoff](stages/s2/handoff_h1.png)

*The handoff study: renderer | glyph, pair by pair, at 14, 12, 10, 8, 7, 6, 5, 4 px. For the bear the glyph is the cleaner lump from 10 px down; for the cougar the renderer keeps the mass and the tail's curl down to 8 px while the glyph is a stick figure above that. So the handoff is per species: bear 10, cougar 8.*

## 6. Two cold critics

**Round 1** (after the first colour ladder and pose sheet). The bear reads at every size, "a black lump" at 12-6 px ("protect it"); the cougar fails from mid size down ("a tan fox or fawn"). Ranked: the tail is a rope to the ground with the black tip "like a hoof"; the legs spindly (Seton draws thick forearms and paws wider than the wrist); ears like posts; hard seams at the joints ("armour plates": the internal masses drawing their own terminators); the bear's back two blobs (the scapula bulging above the back); too much daylight under the bear; poses: the rearing bear "a man in a black coat", the lying bear "a rug", the climbing bear "a bag stuck to a capsule", the salmon a speck, the cat's run "a rocking horse", the pounce's flight "an otter".

![poses p1](stages/s2/poses_p1.png)
![poses p5](stages/s2/poses_p5.png)

*Pose sheets before round 1 and after it: the tail's lowest point off the ground and its tip curled; forearms and paws 30% heavier; the rearing bear slouched at 64° with its belly out and forepaws limp in front; the climb re-solved with paw targets in body coordinates and a trunk; the pounce rebuilt as three keyframes (launch, flight, strike); a salmon 0.85 H long held crosswise.*

Then **Muybridge's plates**, which I'd been unable to fetch all afternoon (Commons returned 429 to every laptop request; a Modal container fetched them in minutes). Plate 720, the cat galloping in 24 frames, showed what the critic's "rocking horse" meant: my cat was stilted with a splayed stride and a stick tail; **Muybridge's cat stays low through the whole gallop, its legs bunched under an arched back in the gathered frames, the tail streaming back**. Stride down to 0.95 H, lifts to 0.22, the body 0.07 H lower.

**Round 2** (after all the sheets). The bear holds; the cougar is still too **light in value** (its value maps near-white where the photos and Seton's drawings are mid-value with a darker back and tail); the tail still under-drawn; the back-light rim ran round the whole body like a sticker border; cubs and kittens were scaled-down adults; the bear's muzzle hooked like a beak; the walking bear glyph at 5-8 px was "a comb". Fixes: the cougar a full step darker with a darker spine band; the tail radius up by half; the rim only on upward-facing fur, never legs; cub and kitten heads 1.3×, cub legs shorter, kitten spots fewer and bolder; a straight bear profile; the bear glyph draws its far legs only from 9 px.

The heads needed their own instrument (`headtest.py`): the cougar's face first read as a sheep (a big pink nose disc, the eye far back, a dark blob on the cheek that was the spine-band rule leaking onto the head).

![heads](stages/s2/heads_h3.png)

## 8. The sheets

- `sheets/B_individual_x_size.png`: 60 → 3 px, landscape and iso.
- `sheets/C_individual_x_facing.png`: 12 yaws, landscape and iso.
- `sheets/D_individual_x_hour.png`: dawn to overcast; golden and dusk back-lit.
- `sheets/E_colour_season_age.png`: black and cinnamon bears, cubs, yearlings through spring, summer and autumn (no winter bear: they den), autumn bears heavier; cougars by season, spotted kittens.
- `sheets/F_gait_cycles.png`: bear walk, pace, gallop; cougar walk, stalk, gallop, pounce, in 8 phases.
- `sheets/G_families.png`: a sow with cubs grazing, in file, treed with the sow at the base; a female with kittens following and at rest; at true relative scale.
- `sheets/H_small_figures.png`: every pose from 12 to 3 px.
- `sheets/anim/*.gif`: bear walk, pace, run, climb; cougar walk, stalk, run, pounce; iso walks at 16 px.

Back-lit.*
